### 3.4 P8: residency you can see, and a CLI that does not fight the dev server

**Change.**

- `client.residency()` returns rows and bytes per table on the device, with
  the store's total bytes and, on the web, the wasm heap. The guide's residency
  card shows it next to the sync windows that bound it.
- `snapback4 run <job>` and the other store-writing CLI commands detect a live
  dev server on the same store (its lifetime lock file) and either run the work
  through that server or refuse with `E_STORE_BUSY`, naming the server's
  address, instead of failing with `database is locked`. Which of the two is
  the lane's first finding.

**Evidence.** Residency on a fixture with known rows; the CLI against a running
dev server, both ways.

**2026-10-08 — `run` goes through a live dev host (app-farm round 1).** The
lane's first finding was the refusal; 27 of ~270 app-farm builds (41 diaries)
then stopped their dev server to run one operation, or reverse-engineered the
wire. `run` now takes the other branch of this item when the owner is a
responsive `snapback4 dev` (it answers `GET /dev/status`): a query or mutation
goes to `POST /q|/m/<op>` with the persona header naming the same principal a
local run would use, and a job to the dev-only `POST /dev/jobs/<job>`, which
queues it behind earlier runs and drives chunks on the dev clock. No second
writer opens the store. `--now`, a non-persona principal, `serve` and a stopped
host still refuse `E_STORE_BUSY`, now with the equivalent `curl`. Other
store-writing commands keep the refusal.

**2026-09-27 — Darwin ownership correction (lane OW).** Ownership now locks
`<database filename>-owner` beside the canonical database, a regular 0600 file
opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`: exclusive for backend writers, shared for devices
including the native C ABI. On macOS/iOS, a flock on the database itself
conflicts with SQLite's POSIX locks, preventing startup adoption and device
commits. Database identity remains `(dev, ino)`, checked with stat alone;
only a missing database is created and closed before SQLite opens it. The
guard holds the sidecar, never a database descriptor whose close could discard
SQLite's locks. Sidecars persist after unlock and move with the database's
other sidecars; symlinks, hard links and nonregular ownership files are refused.
`serve.lock` remains an address hint, and `E_STORE_BUSY` keeps its meaning.

**2026-09-27 — round-1 file-mutation hardening (lane OW).** Both ownership
and address-file opens lstat the database and candidate before opening and
compare the opened inode again before any lock, read, write or chmod. A
post-open alias or uncertain descriptor is deliberately forgotten, never
closed or unlocked: either operation could discard SQLite's POSIX locks.
Ownership sidecars must be empty, and address records at most 128 bytes;
these stat-only bounds also refuse a SQLite database renamed to either name
when its original path is absent. Lock the sidecar before creating a missing
database; an `E_STORE_BUSY` probe creates no database or address record.
Database hard links are refused with `E_STORE` and “copy the store, do not
hard-link it,” including on an existing owner's path check.

**2026-09-27 — round-2 exclusion boundary (lane OW, option b).** Exclusion
holds while `<database>-owner` is left in place. Deleting or replacing it while
a process runs ends exclusion: a second owner can create and lock a new sidecar
while the first can continue reading and writing. Removal does not revoke a
running process or cancel its transactions. The retained descriptor still locks
the old inode, which no longer coordinates new opens. Stop all owners before
moving a store and move its sidecars with it; leave `-owner` in place for the
whole ownership lifetime.

This replaces round 1's partial fail-closed claim and removes its live sidecar
checks. `Store::conn()` exposes a raw SQLite connection: auth autocommit writes,
auth and worker transactions, and effect/action/job/timeline reads bypass the
store's transaction helpers. There is no single existing fallible interception
point; changing that accessor's return type would change the connection API and
its callers, and an accessor check cannot revoke a previously borrowed
connection. No single-digit-microsecond transaction-cost claim is made. The
database inode, hard-link and acquisition-time sidecar checks remain. Unit,
native-device and two-process regressions label unlink/replacement as this
documented boundary and prove a second owner can start while old work continues.

**2026-09-27 — live token mutual proof (lane TK, round 1).** A busy
`token` command asks the announced owner to mint through its existing writer.
The per-run 0600 `<canonical database>-token` secret never crosses HTTP:
BSD wildcard listeners need not own the specific loopback address the CLI
dials. Both rounds require an actual loopback socket peer, one literal
loopback Host, no Origin and single protocol headers. IPv4-mapped IPv6 peers
fail closed, including mapped loopback; native `::1` remains usable.

The client sends a fresh 32-byte OS nonce and the request. The server issues a
fresh 32-byte nonce and HMAC-SHA256 over `snapback4-token-server`, client nonce,
server nonce and request binding. Only after verifying it does the client send
HMAC-SHA256 over `snapback4-token-client`, server nonce, client nonce and the
same binding. Nonces are fixed-width raw bytes within the MAC, hex on the wire;
the HMAC key is the exact 64 ASCII hex bytes in the private file. The binding
is UTF-8 JSON of `[1, service, days, storeIdentity]`; days bind before clamping.
The identity hashes JSON of `[hex(canonicalPathBytes), device, inode]`, avoiding

/bin/bash -lc 'timeout 10s nl -ba snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing.md | head -205' in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Testing: fixtures and the packaged proof kit
     2	
     3	For shared test releases and probe:serve, use `guide staging`: real password users and operator grants on serve. Password-only dev fixture/clock tools require explicit --unsafe-dev-doors; startDev persists unless --memory is explicit.
     4	
     5	Prove backend behavior with fixtures and the rendered UI with the packaged proof kit.
     6	Starters generate frontend:proof and day2:proof, and the kit owns server and browser lifecycles.
     7	
     8	```js
     9	import { startDev, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
    10	const dev = await startDev();
    11	try { console.log(dev.url); } finally { await cleanup(() => dev.stop()); }
    12	```
    13	
    14	Raw HTTP starts with JSON envelopes: POST /q/name `{args:{...}}`, POST /m/name
    15	`{id:"stable-intent-id",args:{...}}`, POST /a/name `{id:"stable-action-id",args:{...}}`.
    16	Keep the same id on an ambiguous retry. A mutation/action id identifies the attempt;
    17	`result.id` identifies a returned row. Query data is under `data`, writes under `result`;
    18	actions have their own lifecycle (`guide actions`), not a mutation receipt.
    19	
    20	The generated frontend:proof builds its own dist with build (web) or build:web (Expo web).
    21	After replacing the starter screen, replace its business assertions too.
    22	`init --adapt` previews only and writes nothing; use the printed `--apply` command.
    23	A custom Home has no write scenario: replace the Home assertions; a proof without a write fails.
    24	`day2:proof` uses versions/v1 and versions/v2 (initial source snapshots and contemporary fixtures).
    25	
    26	E_FIXTURE_ALIAS means a saved alias or path could not resolve. Use an earlier case's `save` name with `$name` or `$name.field`; escape a literal with `$$` or `$literal`. An unresolved assertion fails the case; it never compares the alias as literal data.
    27	
    28	E_FIXTURE_TIME means a relative fixture time is malformed or out of range. Use `now` or `now±duration` with positive integral ms/s/m/h/d/w. Relative conversion applies only to typed time leaves; text/JSON strings stay literal.
    29	
    30	## Refusal ladder
    31	
    32	Supply every valid argument when testing authorization: decode (`E_INPUT`)
    33	runs before the body. For a body ordered lookup → write → require, a hidden
    34	asserted lookup refuses `NOT_FOUND`, a readable denied table write refuses
    35	`E_RULE`, and only then can the later `require` emit its code. After decoding,
    36	statements execute in authored order; put a require first to have it decide
    37	first. Nullable hidden lookups return null; assertions use their authored
    38	`else` code. Hidden denied update/delete targets retain NOT_FOUND. Earlier
    39	writes roll back when a later require refuses.
    40	
    41	A query over hidden rows is complete and empty when the pinned scope uniformly
    42	refuses its viewer. A nonuniform scan can still be partial with no visible
    43	rows. Test the complete flag as well as the count; do not expect E_RULE for a
    44	hidden page. For a compiled role workflow see: `guide rule-recipes role-workflow`.
    45	Use `guide client` for client recipes and the proof helper contracts below.
    46	
    47	## Fixtures and cases
    48	
    49	`test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. A fixture directory has
    50	one `<table>.jsonl` per declared table, each line an object with `id` and fields:
    51	
    52	```jsonl
    53	{"id":"board-alice","owner":"dev:alice","title":"Groceries"}
    54	```
    55	
    56	Persona alice is `dev:alice`, never the literal `alice`. References use row ids;
    57	time is Unix milliseconds or `"now"`, `"now-1h"`, `"now+2d"` in typed time fields
    58	(including optional/list time leaves). Durations are positive whole ms/s/m/h/d/w.
    59	Typed time arguments in cases accept the same forms.
    60	One clock captured at test start anchors these values, seed defaults, cases and
    61	the sweep, so timed fixtures do not rot; malformed/overflowing relative times
    62	refuse E_FIXTURE_TIME. Text and JSON fields retain literal strings.
    63	Money/decimal use integer minor units; bytes are hex; optional fields may be absent/null. Parents load before children.
    64	Defaults, unique keys, caps, maintained values and sequences obey normal insert
    65	semantics: omit sequence and engine-maintained columns. A missing parent is
    66	NOT_FOUND; a bad row or unknown table fails the run before any case executes.
    67	
    68	Add `test.cases.jsonl` to that directory. Cases execute in order; writes change
    69	what later cases see. The complete vocabulary is `as`, `op`, `args`, `expect`,
    70	`data`, `match`, `matchSet`, `count`, `save`, `label`; unknown keys fail before any case runs.
    71	
    72	```jsonl
    73	{"as":"alice","op":"addCard","args":{"board":"board-alice","text":"eggs"},"save":"eggs"}
    74	{"as":"alice","op":"renameCard","args":{"card":"$eggs","text":"milk"}}
    75	{"as":"bob","op":"cardsOn","args":{"board":"board-alice"},"count":0}
    76	```
    77	
    78	`save` names an answer: `$eggs` means its row id, `$eggs.text` its field.
    79	A bare id/reference/principal/cursor/asset argument equal to a saved alias refuses
    80	with the `$name` rewrite (including optional/list leaves). Text, enum and JSON
    81	arguments remain literals. A root `$alias` saved as null refuses E_FIXTURE_ALIAS
    82	by name; `$row.optionalField` may be null.
    83	Bare assertion strings (including enum literals) remain literals even if an alias has that name.
    84	Aliases resolve recursively inside args, data and match. Unknown aliases and
    85	missing fields refuse E_FIXTURE_ALIAS by name. `$$text` means literal
    86	`$text`; `{"$literal":"eggs"}` means literal `eggs`, even if saved as an alias.
    87	`data` asserts the exact result; `match` asserts a recursive object subset
    88	(`"match":{"name":"Ash"}` permits an extra id). Arrays retain their exact
    89	length/order, with each element matched recursively; scalars compare exactly.
    90	`matchSet` compares a list without order: `"matchSet":[{"name":"Ash"},{"name":"Oak"}]`
    91	requires exactly those two recursive subsets, each matched to a different row.
    92	Duplicate counts matter. Nested arrays retain their order. All assertion forms may appear together. `count` asserts length. A mutation defaults to
    93	`sent`; `ok` also accepts a sent mutation and never a refusal; a query defaults to its check verdict (`complete`, or complete/capped when
    94	N_NEVER_COMPLETE). Set `expect` to `complete`, `capped`, `ok`, `denied`, or a
    95	refusal code. `as` must be a declared persona; outsiders need their own persona.
    96	
    97	After the cases, the sweep runs every query with that persona's case arguments when available,
    98	otherwise derives arguments from the store after the cases' writes (text uses
    99	the shortest valid value). Saved-alias arguments belong to the case run; the
   100	sweep uses another literal-argument case or derives arguments for that query.
   101	For different authorization lanes, add `fixtures/test.sweep.json`:
   102	`{"hrStatus":["carol"],"policiesList":["alice","bob"]}`. Each named query uses
   103	its listed personas; other queries use the whole cast. `--as` explicitly overrides
   104	that matrix and names the queries whose sweep personas it overrides. Empty/unknown entries and undeclared personas fail before cases run.
   105	It prints examined rows, entries, probes, steps, heap/output bytes and ceilings.
   106	`complete` is the whole answer; `capped` is a partial answer, not a refusal.
   107	Sweep refusals from a program's own require (family rule) are advisory and do
   108	not fail the run. Other refused sweep queries fail unless that query/persona's
   109	own explicit case expects the same code. This exemption matches query, persona
   110	and code; it does not prove that earlier arguments/state validate the final sweep.
   111	Write one refusal case per refused persona, plus explicit data/count/completeness
   112	assertions for the allowed personas' final state. A refusal case for bob never
   113	covers carol. Failed cases and invalid fixtures exit 1.
   114	N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT means a fixture case no longer fits its operation signature. Update its arguments and run snapback4 test.
   115	
   116	A lookup reads through the caller's rule: hidden rows give NOT_FOUND before
   117	later `require` guards; hidden unique conflicts give E_RULE instead of the
   118	custom code. `test` and dev refusal logs explain the hidden lookup or rule.
   119	`guide refusals` explains refusal ordering and hidden rows.
   120	
   121	## Loading fixture rows for a proof
   122	
   123	```js
   124	import { loadFixtures } from "snapback4/proof";
   125	const rows = await loadFixtures("fixtures", { url: dev.url });
   126	await fetch(`${dev.url}/dev/reset`, {
   127	  method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
   128	  body: JSON.stringify({ seed: rows }),
   129	});
   130	```
   131	
   132	The helper resolves typed relative times against one dev clock from bounded
   133	catalog/status requests (timeoutMs defaults to 10 seconds). Options `now` and
   134	`cwd` override clock/project. Without credentials it reads the dev-only catalog;
   135	`serve` refuses this door. `startDev` also returns `personas`. To authenticate both
   136	reads, pass `{ url: dev.url, persona: dev.personas[0] }`, or `headers` with your
   137	Authorization header. Reload after schema edits. Reset validates rows;
   138	inspect its reply for `denied`. `/seed` and `/dev/reset` accept relative times
   139	directly; `"clock":"reset"` uses the prospective wall clock atomically.
   140	
   141	## An acceptance script over HTTP
   142	
   143	The same kit supports HTTP and browser proofs. HTTP acknowledgement is not React settlement; mutation refusals are `{state:'failed', why}`.
   144	
   145	```js
   146	import assert from "node:assert/strict";
   147	import { startDev, startStatic, withBrowser, fillRN, renderedText,
   148	  waitForFresh, bounded, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
   149	
   150	const dev = await startDev(); // memory store; actual runtime URL
   151	let web;
   152	try {
   153	  web = await startStatic("dist");
   154	  await withBrowser({ executablePath: process.env.CHROME }, async context => {
   155	    const page = await context.newPage();
   156	    await page.goto(`${web.url}/?api=${encodeURIComponent(dev.url)}&witness=web`, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
   157	    await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Continue as a guest", exact: true }).click();
   158	    const title = `Proof ${crypto.randomUUID()}`;
   159	    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Task", { exact: true }), title);
   160	    const button = page.getByRole("button", { name: "Add", exact: true });
   161	    await button.evaluate((element, title) => window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm("add", {title}, element), title);
   162	    await button.click();
   163	    const receipt = await bounded(page.evaluate(() => window.__snapback4Witness.formResult()), 30000, "write receipt");
   164	    assert.equal(receipt.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(receipt));
   165	    await waitForFresh(renderedText(page, title, {within: page.getByRole("list")}), null);
   166	  });
   167	} finally { await cleanup(() => web?.stop(), () => dev.stop()); }
   168	```
   169	
   170	The kit spawns binaries directly, binds the first free port in PORT_LO..PORT_HI
   171	at run time (or an OS-assigned port with neither set), strips ANSI ready lines,
   172	asserts served identity and owns bounded cleanup. Each child logs separate
   173	stdout/stderr files in `<cwd>/logs` by default. These survive the harness's
   174	TMPDIR cleanup (H6); set `logsDir` outside a source-discovery directory if your
   175	tool treats every file under cwd as source (for example, an Exact app/ root).
   176	Set `logsDir` explicitly to retain a chosen artifact directory. `startServe(deployDir)` serves a deployment;
   177	`startVite(dir)` serves the app's Vite config; `startStatic(dir)` serves built
   178	output, including wasm. Spawn servers with the kit or the binary, never `npx`:
   179	killing the npx wrapper leaves the server holding your pipe.
   180	`startDev({args})` still adds --memory unless --store or --no-memory is explicit.
   181	The kit consumes --no-memory to use native dev's persistent `.snapback4/store.sqlite`;
   182	--memory with a persistence flag refuses. --store is forwarded to native validation
   183	(current dev refuses it; choose --no-memory for its project store).
   184	`startDev(args)` and `stopChild(ChildProcess | {url, stop})` remain supported.
   185	`cli(args)` dispatches every published verb through the installed JS wrapper, including web-shell,
   186	awaits close and returns `{code, stdout, stderr, logs}`; assert code
   187	when expecting success. `cli(["token", ...])` never persists stdout: its bearer is
   188	returned in memory only. Use `{secret:true}` with cli/command for other secret
   189	stdout; stderr remains a log, so the invoked tool must keep secrets off stderr.
   190	`withBrowser` automatically adds `witness=web` to top-level HTTP(S) navigations
   191	with an init script before app scripts, including redirect destinations; raw Playwright callers add the flag themselves.
   192	`withBrowser({ chromium })` uses the caller’s Playwright (including @playwright/test);
   193	playwright-core is an optional peer required when using browsers. Chromium launches over
   194	a pipe, with no WebSocket listener. Set CHROME to an installed Chromium; root or a recognized
   195	unavailable sandbox selects --no-sandbox. Ordinary hosts first use the sandbox.
   196	
   197	`fillRN` clears then uses pressSequentially so RN-web gets real keystrokes.
   198	`statusText(page, text)` uses getByText with exact matching: a role=status element
   199	has no content-derived accessible name, so never getByRole('status', {name}).
   200	`selectByValue(locator, value)` uses a stable option value, not a label regex.
   201	Never wait for networkidle: live queries hold long polls. Wait for the control.
   202	
   203	`renderedText(page, text, { exact: true, within: container })` excludes inputs and
   204	textareas. Strings match the entire rendered text by default. Use
   205	`renderedText(page, "verification", {exact:false})` or

```ts
import { startDev, startServe, startStatic, startVite, cli, command, deploy,
  rollback, testSource, withBrowser, waitForFresh, fillRN, statusText,
  selectByValue, renderedText, frontPage, logsDir, bounded, cleanup, stopChild, portRange, sandboxUnavailable, loadFixtures }
  from "snapback4/proof";

export async function applicationProof(cwd: string) {
  const options = { cwd, logsDir: logsDir(), timeoutMs: 120_000 };
  console.log("proof port range", portRange());
  const checked = await cli(["check"], options);
  if (checked.code !== 0) throw Error(checked.stderr);
  const acceptance = await testSource("versions/v1.q", options);
  if (acceptance.code !== 0) throw Error(acceptance.stderr);
  await deploy("deployment", "versions/v1.q", options);
  const backend = await startServe("deployment", options);
  try {
    const build = await command("npm", ["run", "build"], options);
    if (build.code !== 0) throw Error(build.stderr);
    const frontend = await startStatic("dist", options);
    try {
      await withBrowser({ cwd }, async context => {
        const page = await context.newPage();
        await page.goto(`${frontend.url}/?api=${encodeURIComponent(backend.url)}`);
        const status = statusText(page), before = await status.textContent();
        await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Title"), "Proof");
        await selectByValue(page.getByLabel("Category"), "work");
        await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Add" }).click();
        await bounded(waitForFresh(status, before), 35_000, "fresh rendered result");
        await renderedText(page, "Proof", { within: page.getByRole("list") }).waitFor();
        await frontPage(page);
      });
    } finally { await stopChild(frontend); }
    await deploy("deployment", "versions/v2.q", options);
    await rollback("deployment", 1, options);
  } catch (error) {
    console.error(sandboxUnavailable(error) ? "browser sandbox unavailable" : "proof failed");
    throw error;
  } finally { await cleanup(() => backend.stop()); }
}

// Development hosts use the same custody. Add assertions before stopping them.
export async function developmentHosts(cwd: string) {
  const backend = await startDev({ cwd });
  try {
    // Read the application's fixtures/<table>.jsonl using the dev schema/clock.
    const seed = await loadFixtures("fixtures", { url: backend.url, cwd });
    const response = await fetch(`${backend.url}/seed`, { method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify(seed) });
    const result = await response.json();
    if (!response.ok || result.denied) throw Error(JSON.stringify(result));
    const frontend = await startVite(cwd);
    await cleanup(() => frontend.stop());
  } finally { await cleanup(() => backend.stop()); }
}

### Poll authoritative projection after settlement

After asserting a write's sent receipt, a proof can wait for its server-backed
projection. A deadline reports the last answer and never declares a write failed.

```ts
import type { Online } from "snapback4/app";
import { waitForServer } from "snapback4/proof";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export async function committedNote(online: Online, id: string) {
  return waitForServer(() => online.query(api.staffNotes, {team:"proof"}),
    read => "data" in read && read.fresh && read.data.some(row => row.id === id),
    {label:"committed note"});
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
## 8. The client, dev and rule seams the runs hit

Each item names the loop it removes (§2.2).

1. **A mutation's result reaches the client.** `Write` gains
   `result` on `sent` (the server already answers with it,
   `crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs`); `useMutation().run()` resolves with
   the `Write`. Ruled (2026-09-13): `result` on `sent` only — no
   `predicted` on `pending`; a prediction is visible as `pending` rows,
   not as an answer.
   Fresh replies remove Grok's DM-reuse lookup. **2026-09-14 correction
   under LLP 3000.008's delegated program:** §17 deliberately withholds
   historical values on replay. `Write<R>` therefore distinguishes fresh
   `sent` with `result: R` (including null) from `sent` with
   `replayed: true` and no result. Both clients and hooks preserve this
   distinction; a lost acknowledgement never grants historical read
   authority or justifies resubmission with a new ID. An application may
   make a newly authorized query when it receives execution identity only.
2. **A shape over a device row keeps `pending`.** `*` spreads device
   metadata through as it spreads columns; `api.ts` types a shaped
   object with `pending?: boolean` when its base is a row. Removes the
   0.2.6 M6 loop and the `historyRows` second query.
3. **`webDevice()` persists the wasm.** The opener keeps the module's
   bytes in IndexedDB by digest and opens on them with no network, so an
   offline reload does not depend on a URL. Removes the M3 loop the
   builder solved by hand and makes the cold-offline promise the
   opener's, not the app's.
4. **The `{ id }` asset argument predicts.** The device resolves the
   uploader's own upload facts onto the argument as the server does
   (`issues/20260913-snapback4-video-id-only-prediction-refuses.md`).
5. **Identity admission under `dev`.** Ruled (2026-09-13): on loopback,
   `snapback4 dev` lifts the per-peer and per-email minute counters to
   what the host can carry — the Argon2 slots and the connection caps,
   which are the physical bounds, remain — and prints the fact once;
   `serve` is unchanged. Removes two loops in each of the Astra and
   Grok lanes and the harness's backoff scaffolding.
   **2026-10-06, HR:** password seed scripts and replay adapters use the
   existing `signup`/`login` exports from `snapback4/auth`, passing the returned
   session to one `createClient` per account. Signup already creates a live
   session. No new auth verb, credential store or development bypass is needed.
   The auth card and starter READMEs teach this; the social starter emits a
   Node script for N accounts and each account's authorized profile write.
   Reruns choose login explicitly; arbitrary signup refusals never trigger an
   automatic fallback. Tests execute the published guide and installed script,
   including fresh login, authorized writes and cross-user refusal. Existing
   Argon2, admission limits, schema opt-in and dev-only doors stay unchanged.
6. **A hidden unique clash says what it is.** The refusal stays generic
   `E_RULE` where it must (§8.7), and its message adds the one sentence
   from `why E_RULE`: "or a unique key collided with a row you cannot
   read; to write into another principal's row use `upsert` under an
   `update` rule, or let a `maintain` keep the count."
7. **Write authority is the write rule (revised after the blind review,
   §15).** What Clique's counter tables hit is the binder: a unique-key
   `update` or `upsert` reads its target under the READ rule first
   (`bind/program.rs:603–641`), so a hidden target looks absent — an
   alternate-key update is `NOT_FOUND`, an upsert inserts and collides
   with generic `E_RULE` — while a by-id `update` already fetches without
   a read check and applies the update rule (`write.rs:66–100`). Two
   lanes opened their tables to get past it. The proposal, narrowed to
   what both reviews found defensible:
   (a) **target resolution is engine-private**: a unique-key or id write
   resolves its target without the read rule, then the table's `update`
   rule decides, as Postgres applies an `UPDATE` policy without a
   `SELECT` policy. **The update rule judges old and next:** an unnamed
   `update <- R` must hold for the row before the write (the pre-write
   state) and for the merged row after it, `R[old] and R[next]`, as an
   `UPDATE` policy's `USING` judges the existing row and, with no
   `WITH CHECK`, the new row; a named `update (old, next)` judges exactly
   what it names. A rule that reads only `id` and immutable columns is
   judged once, because a patch cannot change its inputs (a hidden target
   drops immutable patch fields; a readable mismatch is refused). A denial
   follows (b): `E_RULE` on a readable target, `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable
   one. *Fixed 2026-10-08:* until then the engine judged only the next
   image, so `update <- .owner = viewer` without `immutable owner` let any
   caller take another principal's row by writing `owner: viewer`, even a
   row it could not read. The Lean oracle found it (LLP 3000.035 §5.2,
   `lean/OPEN.md` R2); the fix is `schema/update_images.rs`, applied in
   `check_rule` on every host;
   (b) **one failure word for an unreadable target**: absence, an update
   rule that refuses, a failed `if` condition, an immutable-column
   mismatch and an incomplete patch on the insert path all answer
   `NOT_FOUND` — the zero-row update — with no `current`, no named index
   and no named column; a readable target keeps today's codes. The
   disclosure granted is exactly: a row the update rule let this writer
   modify exists, and the rule's predicates held of it;
   (c) **the merged image never leaves the engine**: a valued write's
   `result` and `E_CONFLICT`'s `current` are a read-rule `get` (null when
   hidden), never the merged row; `old` in `update (old, next)` is the
   engine's, bound nowhere in the author's program; asset facts on
   unchanged columns are preserved without re-validation, and the update
   rule is checked before any asset diagnostic (`write.rs:75–156`);
   (d) **withheld before recorded**: `_ops` stores exactly the body `/m`
   returns, so a replay cannot grow a row (the existing gap is
   `issues/20260913-snapback4-ops-replay-returns-cached-content-after-revocation.md`);

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#3-proof-fixture-loading — only schema-typed time leaves resolve relative strings.
import { readFile, readdir } from "node:fs/promises";
import { resolve, join } from "node:path";

export interface FixtureRow { table: string; row: Record<string, unknown> }
export interface LoadFixturesOptions {
  /** A running dev host, used for its public field types and current clock. */
  url: string;
  cwd?: string;
  /** Authenticate catalog and clock reads when needed. */
  persona?: string;
  headers?: Record<string, string>;
  /** Override the captured dev clock for a deterministic proof. */
  now?: number;
  timeoutMs?: number;
}
/** Read <table>.jsonl files, excluding cases; return one reset/seed-ready batch.
 * Schema and clock requests are bounded. Nothing is written to the server.
 */
export async function loadFixtures(directory: string, options: LoadFixturesOptions): Promise<FixtureRow[]> {
  const timeoutMs = options.timeoutMs ?? 10_000;
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(timeoutMs) || timeoutMs <= 0) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: loadFixtures timeoutMs must be a positive integer");
  const headers = new Headers(options.headers);
  if (options.persona !== undefined) headers.set("x-snapback-persona", options.persona);
  const read = async (path: string) => {
    const response = await fetch(new URL(path, options.url), { headers, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs) });
    const body = await response.json() as Record<string, unknown>;
    if (response.status === 401 || response.status === 403 || (body.denied as { code?: string } | undefined)?.code === "E_AUTH")
      throw Error("E_AUTH: loadFixtures could not read the catalog; pass { persona } or { headers }");
    if (!response.ok || body.denied) throw Error(`E_INPUT: ${path}: ${JSON.stringify(body)}`);
    return body;
  };
  const [catalog, status] = await Promise.all([read(options.persona !== undefined || options.headers !== undefined ? "/" : "/dev/catalog"), options.now === undefined ? read("/dev/status") : Promise.resolve({ now: options.now })]);
  const now = status.now;
  if (typeof now !== "number" || !Number.isSafeInteger(now) || now < 0) throw Error("E_FIXTURE_TIME: dev did not report a valid clock");
  const tables = catalog.tables as Array<{ name: string; fields: Record<string, unknown> }>;
  if (!Array.isArray(tables)) throw Error("E_INPUT: host did not report table field types");
  const dir = resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", directory), rows: FixtureRow[] = [];
  for (const name of (await readdir(dir)).filter(name => name.endsWith(".jsonl") && name !== "test.cases.jsonl").sort()) {
    const table = name.slice(0, -6), definition = tables.find(item => item.name === table);
    if (!definition) throw Error(`E_INPUT: ${name}: unknown table ${table}`);
    const lines = (await readFile(join(dir, name), "utf8")).split(/\r?\n/);
    for (let n = 0; n < lines.length; n++) {
      if (!lines[n]!.trim()) continue;
      const at = `${name}:${n + 1}`;
      let row: unknown;
      try { row = JSON.parse(lines[n]!); } catch (error) { throw Error(`E_INPUT: ${at}: ${error}`); }
      if (!row || typeof row !== "object" || Array.isArray(row)) throw Error(`E_INPUT: ${at}: expected a row object`);
      const fields = row as Record<string, unknown>;
      for (const [field, kind] of Object.entries(definition.fields)) {
        if (field in fields) fields[field] = timeValue(kind, fields[field], now, `${at}.${field}`);
      }
      rows.push({ table, row: fields });
    }
  }
  return rows;
}
function timeValue(kind: unknown, value: unknown, now: number, at: string): unknown {
  if (kind && typeof kind === "object") {
    const type = kind as Record<string, unknown>;
    if ("Optional" in type) return value === null ? null : timeValue(type.Optional, value, now, at);
    const list = (type.List ?? type.ListRange) as { of?: unknown } | undefined;
    if (list && Array.isArray(value)) return value.map((item, i) => timeValue(list.of, item, now, `${at}[${i}]`));
  }
  if (kind !== "Time" || typeof value !== "string" || !value.startsWith("now")) return value;
  const match = /^(?:now|now([+-])([0-9]+)(ms|s|m|h|d|w))$/.exec(value);
  if (!match) throw Error(`E_FIXTURE_TIME: ${at}: ${JSON.stringify(value)}: expected now or now±positive integer ms/s/m/h/d/w`);
  const units: Record<string, bigint> = { ms: 1n, s: 1000n, m: 60000n, h: 3600000n, d: 86400000n, w: 604800000n };
  const delta = match[2] === undefined ? 0n : BigInt(match[2]) * units[match[3]!]!;
  const resolved = BigInt(now) + (match[1] === "-" ? -delta : delta);
  if ((match[2] !== undefined && delta === 0n) || resolved < 0n || resolved > BigInt(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER)) throw Error(`E_FIXTURE_TIME: ${at}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} is outside the supported duration/time range`);
  return Number(resolved);
}

/// Created before worker admission; aborted admission counts without a header.
/// Accepted time originates before HTTP body prebuffering.
pub(super) struct Sample { registry: Arc<Registry>, path: String, accepted: Instant, timing: Option<Timing>, headers: bool }
impl Sample {
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub fn new(request: &Request, registry: &Arc<Registry>) -> Option<Self> {
        Self::with_headers(request, registry, false)
    }
    pub fn with_headers(request: &Request, registry: &Arc<Registry>, headers: bool) -> Option<Self> {
        #[cfg(test)]
        if !registry.2.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) { return None; }
        let path = request.url().split('?').next().unwrap_or("");
        let path = match (request.method(), path) {
            (Method::Post, "/sync") | (Method::Get, "/changes") => path,
            (Method::Post, "/assets") => "/assets (upload)",
            (Method::Get, path) if path.starts_with("/assets/") => "/assets (download)",
            (Method::Post, path) if ["/q/", "/m/", "/a/"].iter().any(|prefix| path.starts_with(prefix)) => path,
            _ if headers => "", // Timing on other dev responses, no new registry names.
            _ => return None,
        };
        Some(Self { registry: registry.clone(), path: path.to_owned(), accepted: request.accepted_at(), timing: None, headers })
    }
    pub fn run(mut self, handle: impl FnOnce()) {
        let timing = Timing { accepted: self.accepted, wait: Duration::ZERO, engine: Duration::ZERO,
            engine_started: None, depth: 0, acquiring: false, cpu_start: thread_cpu(), headers: self.headers, finished: None };
        CURRENT.with(|current| { assert!(current.get().is_none()); current.set(Some(timing)); });
        struct Clear<'a>(&'a mut Sample);
        impl Drop for Clear<'_> { fn drop(&mut self) {
            finished(true);
            self.0.timing = CURRENT.with(|current| current.take());
        } }
        let _clear = Clear(&mut self);
        handle();
    }
}
impl Drop for Sample {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let (total, refused, cpu, engine) = self.timing.and_then(|t| t.finished)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| (self.accepted.elapsed(), true, None, Duration::ZERO));
        let wait = self.timing.map_or(Duration::ZERO, |t| t.wait);
        self.registry.record(&self.path, self.accepted, total, wait, engine, cpu, refused);
    }
}
// Finish before automatic response/body cleanup on an unanswered handler.
pub(super) struct FinishOnDrop;
impl Drop for FinishOnDrop { fn drop(&mut self) { finished(true); } }

/// Reader/writer ownership, including authentication and response-head reads.
/// Nested ownership counts once; no span includes a wait for a change poll.
pub(super) struct EngineSpan(bool);
impl EngineSpan {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self(CURRENT.with(|current| {
            let Some(mut timing) = current.get().filter(|t| t.finished.is_none()) else { return false; };
            if timing.depth == 0 && !timing.acquiring { timing.engine_started = Some(Instant::now()); }
            timing.depth += 1;
            current.set(Some(timing)); true
        }))
    }
}
impl Drop for EngineSpan {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if !self.0 { return; }
        CURRENT.with(|current| {
            if let Some(mut timing) = current.get() {
                timing.depth -= 1;
                if timing.depth == 0 && let Some(started) = timing.engine_started.take() { timing.engine += started.elapsed(); }
                current.set(Some(timing));
            }
        });
    }
}
// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.7 — reader acquisition is wait even inside writer ownership.
// Nested acquisition helpers count once; resume the enclosing engine span only
// after opening/checking out the reader, including failed opens and unwinding.
pub(super) fn acquire<T>(open: impl FnOnce() -> T) -> T {
    struct Acquisition(Option<Instant>);
    impl Drop for Acquisition {
        fn drop(&mut self) {
            let Some(started) = self.0 else { return; };
            CURRENT.with(|current| {
                if let Some(mut timing) = current.get() {
                    timing.wait += started.elapsed();
                    timing.acquiring = false;
                    if timing.depth > 0 { timing.engine_started = Some(Instant::now()); }
                    current.set(Some(timing));
                }
            });
        }
    }
    let _acquisition = Acquisition(CURRENT.with(|current| {
        let Some(mut timing) = current.get().filter(|t| !t.acquiring && t.finished.is_none()) else { return None; };
        if let Some(started) = timing.engine_started.take() { timing.engine += started.elapsed(); }
        timing.acquiring = true;
        current.set(Some(timing));
        Some(Instant::now())
    }));
    open()

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

A track that passes every gate is a candidate for promotion. A track that
fails the loop bar or acquisition is killed at the clock unless the author
rules otherwise. A mixed result names the gains and the remaining costs, and
the author decides which substrates move into a survivor — which may be
Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000 §13).

**Clock and checkpoints.** Week 1: floors, experience prototype, simulator
skeleton, first publish. Week 2: server core, partition stream, meter,
simulator invariants. Week 3: web replica, outbox, local queries, the chat
app on the real client. Week 4: Expo, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop
bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record. Four weeks from the first
commit.

## 4. Fence, numbering, routing

- **Directory.** `snapback4/`, with its own Cargo workspace and Bun
  workspace, excluded from the root workspace as `snapback2/` is. No
  Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 gate, inventory, backstop, registry or script
  applies; the pre-push hook selects none.
- **Identities.** Binary and npm shim `snapback4`; data directory
  `.snapback4/`; ephemeral ports; its own personas, credentials and process
  ownership. It never depends at runtime on Snapback 2 internals. It copies
  source deliberately, split under the 1,500-line cap on the way in.
- **Numbering.** LLPs 3000 and up; sub-LLPs `3000.NNN`; `llp-create
  --under 3000`. The 2000 series remains Snapback 3's and the 1000 series
  Snapback 2's. Design work for Snapback 4 extends this root or LLP 2000.000
  before it creates a new number.
- **Rules.** `rules/RULES.md` binds "LLPs numbered 1000 and up" by its
  letter, as LLP 2000 §9 noted for its own series. Snapback 4 adopts its
  budgets and loop shape as its own: five blocking checks in sixty seconds,
  `check` under a second, `doctor` under half a second, 1,500 lines per
  file, a bounded working set, delete rather than deprecate, only
  implemented specs bind. Its not-doing list is `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`,
  written with the directory. The root `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` routing line
  and the rules fence note land with the directory, not with this document.
- **Publication.** From week one, `0.0.x`, one script; the benchmark runs
  the published pin.

NOT READY

One material finding remains in commit `bda003c332f5f36b85ecc70d4b4ddc491dde9b96`.

**[P2] Persistent `startDev` migration leaves published fixture proofs operating on the developer’s store.**

[startDev](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof.ts:58) now correctly defaults to persistent storage, but published callers still depend on its former isolation:

- [developmentHosts](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md:4052) starts default dev, then submits fixtures through system-authorized `/seed`. It now upserts into the existing `.snapback4/store.sqlite`, potentially overwriting development rows. On password-only schemas, its fixture reads instead fail because doors are closed.
- [The testing guide](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing.md:150) still labels `startDev()` a memory store. [Its helper contract](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing.md:180) explicitly promises automatic `--memory`. This incorrectly teaches isolation beside the guide’s destructive fixture-reset recipe.

**Repair:** Make disposable acceptance and fixture examples explicitly pass `--memory`, plus `--unsafe-dev-doors` where fixture tools are required. Replace the obsolete automatic-memory contract. Add a regression that runs the published fixture recipe beside a prepopulated persistent dev store and verifies that store remains unchanged.

The generated frontend proof migration and historical digest adaptation are present. I found no additional material defect in the inspected staging classification, production refusal, operator first-admin boundary, token capture, or queued authority revalidation paths.

This finding concerns the committed executable documentation and caller migration. Packed/platform qualification and test execution remain separate evidence; I did not invoke Cargo, run tests, or modify files.
